# Alpha, Beta, Thematic Biweekly – The Return of M&A and AI's Impact on Results

In this biweekly briefing, we explore the return of M&A in the region, results and reactions by AI category, signals from China's NPC, and HK property easing. Market performance, valuations, flows and positioning metrics also included.

## Key Takeaways

■ Market Views: Japan and India remain top DM and EM picks, alongside OWs on Mexico, Poland, Greece and Singapore.

■ Sector Views: We like Semis/Equipment into an expected 2024 recovery as well as Financials in India and Indonesia, and Industrials in India and Mexico.

Key Themes: We like AI Enablers and Adopters, Japanese Corporate Reform, and select China SOE and Korea Value opportunities.

Flows and Positioning: Flows have picked up to China via passive vehicles, with active US/EU investors still selling. Japan has seen buying but remains an UW.

Exhibit 1: Chart of the Fortnight - Earnings & Revenue Surprises and 2 Day Reactions post 4Q23 Earnings Announcements for AI Categories show Enablers meeting high expectations, but little reaction to misses for Disrupted and Adopter companies
![](images/242d33a90c363354dc5b1cf413af81c53194270e8d4393399abfee8738f08505.jpg)
Source: MSCI, Bloomberg, RIMES, FactSet, Morgan Stanley Research Data as of Mar 1, 2024

Top of the Agenda: Incoming policy details from China's NPC have missed consensus expectations on fiscal support and remain supply-centric, increasing the risk of a debt-deflation loop and reinforcing our caution on China equities. Meanwhile, we see a return of M&A globally, with Japan (including MBOs) and Australia in focus in the region. We also explore how AI is impacting the ongoing

## Top of the Agenda

## Daniel Blake, Kristal Ji

This fortnight we tackle:

• The Return of M&A - Where Are We Focused

• How is AI Impacting Asian Earnings?

• Japan on Track to Hit Our Base Case Target

• China NPC - Fiscal Package Insufficient

• HK Budget - Positive for Selective Developers though Macro Outlook Remains Challenged

## The Return of M&A - Where Are We Focused

We explored the outlook for M&A, bringing in perspectives across our global equity and credit strategists, as well as a survey of our analysts spanning 150 sectors (see Global Equity & Credit Strategy: The Return of M&A).

Following a deep downturn in 2023, we expect a 50% cyclical recovery of M&A volumes in 2023. Leading indicators are flashing green, including banks pointing to deal pipelines building, while headwinds to corporate confidence ease. Our survey of Morgan Stanley Research analysts sees activity rising in half of 150 covered industries globally (and only declining in 6%).

Exhibit 2: Global M&A volume dropped sharply last year. We see volumes up 50% in 2024, and tailwinds to a multi-year secular recovery

![](images/b7e2ced19163b6042ef0eeaabd85ebc932e1c790ef52d8dd616651d9ac0b41ee.jpg)
Source: Dealogic, Morgan Stanley Research forecasts; Note: Data exclude rejected/cancelled deals and include carve-out deals.

The structural case is driven by dry powder, private markets, regional shifts and new innovations. 2021-23 may have seen US\$4-11 trillion less activity than macro fundamentals would suggest, supporting a multi-year catch-up. Regionally, we see a 'drought' ending in Europe and Australia, while Japan continues a structural shift towards greater corporate efficiency and activity. Necessity and opportunity should drive more M&A in non-listed privates, where 1,200+ unicorns are currently valued at US\$4 billion+ and private equity-backed names look for exits. Global listed non-financials hold US\$5.6 trillion in cash while private market investors sit on US\$2.5 trillion of dry powder, providing fuel for activity. And rising demand for AI capabilities, the clean energy transition, innovation in life sciences, reshoring and geographic diversification in a multipolar world should structurally support M&A over the next cycle.

In Japan, we think that there will be a trend to gradually rising M&A activity with an outbound skew as Japanese corporations begin to expand their global reach. Our analyst survey indicated seven sectors where our bottom-up teams expect increased activity over the next 12 months (Beverages, Pharmaceuticals, Financials, Transportation, Retail, J-Reits and Logistics). In five of those, a strong desire for growth was identified by our analysts as the most important factor. We are also watchful of a potential increase in management buyout (MBO) activity, supported by recent Tokyo Stock Exchange reforms and the release of METI's Guidelines for Corporate Takeovers (see Japan Equity Strategy: Bracing for Potential TOB/MBO Wave, February 28, 2024).

Australia has also seen a sharp pick-up in M&A announcements, with several large transactions flagged in just the past few weeks (#1, #2, #3). Our Australia Macro+ team had flagged M&A activity as likely to pick up through 2024 as funding stability, optimism in an earnings cycle trough and a low AUD made acquisitions a more attractive prospect – particularly for offshore buyers. They also examine the features that make companies an attractive target, in particular: 1) Value (trading cheap relative to history); 2) Capex cycle (past peak capex spending); 3) Ownership (less restrictive share register); and 4) Industry activity (deal intensity within a sector can often drive additional activity). For more details, see Australia Macro Matters: 2024 Key Theme - Ending The M&A Drought, February 6, 2024.

Exhibit 3: APxJ M&A deal count and volume – on a downward trend and no recovery in sight
![](images/5f15b1f77770668cdde6398ea8d309161e9d82ad6f084806aaecc9ee1e2bfd5c.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, Morgan Stanley Research

The M&A picture overall in Asia ex Japan is in sharp contrast to that of Japan and Australia, with deal count and volumes currently exceptionally low by historical standards with no evidence of recovery at the end of 2023 and into 2024. The biggest drag on activity is China/Hong Kong, which have dominated historically. This is due to the confluence of a meaningful slowdown in nominal GDP growth in China as well as heightened regulatory activity in a wide range of strategic sectors – both as it impacts onshore activity but also in relation to cross-border transactions.

## How is AI Impacting Asian Earnings?

Amid ongoing market leadership of semiconductors and the AI supply chain, we look more broadly at how 4Q23 results have played out across our AI mapping categories (Enablers, Adopters, Disrupted, Wildcard and Don't Know) as laid out in Asia EM Equity Strategy: Mapping AI Adoption in APAC (8 Feb 2024). This follows our more conventional sector/ market breakdown reported in Asia EM Equity Strategy: APxJ/EM 4Q23 Results - Solid Innings from Australia, India, Korea and Mexico (4 Mar 2024).

• Enablers Beat Estimates, but not Market Expectations - Across Asia and EM equities, our Enabler cohort of companies delivered an average modest earnings beat of +2.2% in 4Q23 on net income, although stock reaction was muted following strong preceding returns.

• Adopters Missed, but this was also Anticipated - Market performance of Adopters in Asia/EM since January 2022 has generally been poor outside of Japan, and 4Q23 saw an average earnings miss of 9.2%, although little market reaction.

• Enabler+Adopter and Protected companies saw in-line results with little reaction.

• Wildcard and Disrupted companies also missed analyst estimates, but the lack of a market reaction suggests that in aggregate this was expected.

![](images/f49825754de8c7d6e559662bebef9538848cd637a457cd382103a3987eee8a7d.jpg)
Source: MSCI, Bloomberg, RIMES, FactSet, Morgan Stanley Research Data as of Mar 1, 2024

Overall we have continued to position our Focus Lists towards core Enablers, such as TSMC, SK Hynix, Samsung Electronics, Mediatek and Tokyo Electron. However, as valuations for these companies move up towards the higher end of historical ranges, we continue to look for underappreciated beneficiaries in the Enabler+Adopter and Adopter cohorts, and highlight Recruit Holdings in Japan and Nu Holdings (NuBank) and MercadoLibre in Latin America as other inclusions as examples. As a reminder, a complete list of Adopters and Enablers is available upon request.

## Japan on Track to Hit Our Base Case Target

Nikkei has completed its 34-year round trip and standing firmly well above the peak made in December 1989, and TOPIX has made a bull run of 14.4% YTD as well. TOPIX currently trades at 15.0x 12mf P/E which is our base case forecast for December 2024, so now further upside will mainly come from earnings growth potential in 2024-2025, which we estimate to be at 9%. We see 3.5% upside from spot to our base-case December 2024 2,800 target. Further earnings growth may come from reflation-driven top-line and margin expansion, or corporate reforms to enhance shareholder returns.

Top-down, our Japan economist Yamaguchi Takeshi maintains his base case of an NIRP exit in March (BoJ Monetary Policy Meeting scheduled March 18-19), and upcoming spring wage negotiations (around March 15) remain the key to watch to assess the underlying reflation strength in Japan. While the market was surprised by the 'technical recession' indicated by the preliminary 4Q23

GDP print, we think this overstates the weakness on the consumer side in particular, and expect this to be revised to positive growth on March 11 (see Japan Economics: Large Upward Revision to 4Q GDP Likely: Tailwind for March NIRP Removal (MoF Corp Stats and Revised 4Q GDP Preview).

On the bottom-up front, three non-life insurance companies in Japan have also announced plans to eliminate strategic stockholdings, reconfirming the corporate reforms progress across the board. The unwind will lead to short-term boost to profits and payout amounts, but more importantly it may be a starting point to drive better asset efficiency and free up resources to enhance shareholder returns. In Japan's General Machinery space (see the recent initiation report here), some companies are gradually reducing their cyclicality and improving their business structure and earnings growth sustainability, laying a foundation for multiples to re-rate from a pure cyclical play. Finally, our view of improving shareholder return policies in the real estate and construction sector has seen an early demonstration with Obayashi lifting its dividend-on-equity policy from \~3% to \~5%, driving a re-rating across the sector. We highlight Kajima and Sumitomo Realty & Development in our Japan Focus List as likely beneficiaries of this theme (see Japan Equity Strategy ).

The largest risk to Japan equities' ongoing leadership from here remains a sudden downturn in the US economy, given the double-exposure through direct operating revenues (16% of total Japan revenues are sourced from the US) and FX sensitivity to a stronger JPY (around 0.5% for every ¥1 move against USD). However, our baseline remains for a US soft-landing and so the more likely risk is for a period of muted headline returns as the index has shown a pattern over the last 5 years of quick level shifts higher followed by 1-4 quarters of consolidation.

Alpha strategies in Japan revolve around a preference for domestic non-manufacturing companies, particularly those with a corporate reform angle. One example is the real estate development and construction sector, where we have been expecting a lift in shareholder returns. Another is in the non-bank financial space, where we have seen nonlife insurers adopt a target of unwinding all strategic shareholdings. Our quant strategy team's preference for small-mid cap value is fully aligned with this outlook, as well as our broader coverage.

## China NPC - Fiscal Package Insufficient

China NPC (National People's Congress) outcomes imply an almost flat augmented deficit compared to last year (11.9% of GDP in 2024 versus 11.8% in 2023), as the fiscal package (indicated in green bars in Exhibit 6 ) still does not indicate major expansion, largely offset by the contraction in LGFV borrowing and land sales. Beijing may increase the deficit at mid-year Politburo meetings, but it still likely to remain insufficient to boost the economy. In addition, the GDP growth target is set to 5% with CPI target around 3% (see our China Economists' takes here).

From the Asia/EM equity strategy side, we hold a cautious view on China equities, and think a more comprehensive package is needed to reflate the economy and boost market sentiment in a sustainable manner. MSCI China trades at 8.7x 12mf P/E though downward earnings revision pressure remains, therefore it still does not indicate a good entry point. Our China Equity Strategy Team recommends alpha capturing and holds a preference for A shares over offshore China equities, while reiterating the multi-year investment theme of SOE reform, the beneficiaries of which are summarized in China Equity Strategy: FAQs on Our Recent China SOE Reform Report (4 Mar 2024).

Exhibit 6: China's 2024 Budget Report Suggests Little Expansion in Augmented Fiscal Deficit
![](images/1fdbf647c288761074d9e712ee98590f28fd74137953a0f38c353e8ee84628cb.jpg)
Source: CEIC, MoF, Morgan Stanley China Economics Research estimates

## HK Budget - Positive for Selective Developers though Macro Outlook Remains Challenged

Following the scrapping of all cooling measures for home purchases in Hong Kong, our Hong Kong Property Analyst Praveen expects HK residential prices to go up by 5% in 2024 (versus a prior forecast of a 10% decline) and expects the easing will drive transaction volumes. Nonetheless a weak GDP growth outlook in Hong Kong, deflationary pressure in China and higher-for-longer rates continue to be headwinds for the sector in general, and Praveen prefers well-capitalized companies rather than the whole sector (see Hong Kong Property: Buyer Count Surges [28 Feb 2024] for more details).

Our HK Banks analyst Nick Lord views the change as only a marginal positive for HK banks as it lowers some of the restrictions on property lending, if and when demand does improve. However, mortgage pricing remains tight and banks still need to go through credit risk assessments related to loans. Our HK/China Economist Jenny Zheng maintains her below-consensus GDP forecast for Hong Kong of 0.5% given the fiscal consolidation which may weigh on households and corporates, despite the property easing measures.

We do not view the news as significant in our market allocation framework and remain equal-weight on Hong Kong, which ranks #15 among 27 markets. Macro headwinds persist with negative earnings revisions, and property easing measures have not proven to be a sustainable method to boost the economy.

## Asia EM Flows and Positioning

## Kristal Ji

• Aggregate flows (EPFR, to February 28, 2024): EM and China reversed to outflows, and active long-only US and EU funds were still selling Chinese equities ( Exhibit 38 ).

• Flows by market (EPFR, to February 28, 2024): Relative to AUM, India, Turkey and Mexico recorded the largest inflows within EM, while China, Thailand and Brazil were recording the largest outflows. ( Exhibit 37 ).

• Japan flows (JPX, to February 23, 2024): Japan foreign inflows have broadly come back since the end of October 2023, having net bought US\$12.0bn YTD 2024. Retailers have been net sellers YTD in aggregate and net sold US\$7.2bn YTD 2024 ( Exhibit 39 and Exhibit 40 ).

• Asia/EM Positioning (EPFR, to January 2024): India underweight in GEM funds allocation widened given managers have not yet caught up with the rising index weight ( Exhibit 3 ). China is seeing dropping benchmark weight and GEM funds managers' allocation, with 91bp underweight vs. benchmark ( Exhibit 4 ). Japan underweight in global long-only equity funds widened to 0.44% as of end-January mainly due to index benchmark weights rising faster than actual portfolio allocation, and we continue to expect Japan to eventually come to overweight ( Exhibit 42 ).

## Asia Quant Strategy

## Gilbert Wong, Jason Ng

## Key Views

• The very different fund flows dynamics in APxJ/EM vs. Japan led to a decoupling of Momentum factor performance in these two markets.

• We observed a broad style rotation away from "Momentum" to "High Beta" stocks in APxJ/EM.

• Momentum trade in Japan is highly correlated with "US Momentum," "High Japan Beta," and "Large Cap Value" exposures.

• We recommend "Quality Growth" in APxJ/EM and "Small-Mid Cap Value" in Japan to improve Sharpe ratios.

Latest market dynamics: Price action in APxJ/EM markets has reflected a significant reversal basis over the past two weeks, with "Price Momentum" factor losing 4.4%. Realized Momentum factor volatility was highest among major style categories, sinking its risk-adjusted return from the best-performing style in January to the weakest in February MTD. In Japan, Momentum stocks continued to lead the gains, with Large Cap Low Quality Value stocks standing out. Still, its factor volatility also rose quickly and is ranked among the top volatile styles.

Exhibit 43: L/S factor return of major styles and factors – APxJ/EM equities
![](images/b296329b2442294e6e30949a66a4e494a923cfdc4406950212f38fc47087bb3a.jpg)
Source: FactSet, Morgan Stanley Research; data as of February 23, 2024; Past performance is no guarantee of future result

Our thoughts: Per our last Asia Quant Biweekly, we suggested trimming Momentum exposure in Asia/EM to manage risk of a potential factor reversal. The reversal is now evolving in APxJ/EM, but not yet in Japan. We continued to observe a high and rising stock correlation among outperformers with declining market breadth. Momentum factor risk is increasing. History suggests a Momentum reversal usually happens first in Asia/EM, followed by the US ( Exhibit 44 ). Thus, we reiterate our stance on controlling risk around Momentum exposure in Asia/EM.

Exhibit 44: The classic sequential reversal of "Momentum Factor" from APxJ/EM/ Japan to US.
![](images/5ff6a64e4a106e6a83d766e2fc3d1f990acc2afe65368ca13bb1685db4fd6b95.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, Morgan Stanley Research; data coverage: Jan 1999 to Dec 2001. Notes: the momentum factor performance was calculated based on MSCI style index. Factor performance is indexed to be 100 on 27th March 2000, which is the peak of Nasdaq 100 index in 2000.

The very different fund flow dynamics in APxJ/EM vs. Japan: From a market microstructure perspective, the key driver behind Momentum factor is all about "fund flows." Continuous inflows to, or outflows from a market could lead Price Momentum factor to outperform, and the direction of fund flows could determine whether Price Momentum would be correlated with High Beta or Low Beta stocks, respectively.

Looking at the Asia/EM region, Momentum factor was the best performing style in both APxJ/EM and Japan markets. However, Momentum stocks in Japan were correlated with High Beta and Large-cap Value stocks. The correlation with High Beta stocks in Japan was driven mainly by the strong net inflows from foreigners, while the outperformance of Large-cap Value stocks was helped by JPY depreciation and corporate reform momentum.

In APxJ/EM, fund flows have been with occasional net outflows. Momentum stocks were correlated with "Low Beta" exposure the most. As overall fund flows in APxJ/EM have been relatively marginal, our model estimates a relatively higher reversal risk of Momentum factor there than that in Japan.

Exhibit 45: The very different fund flows (US\$m) in Japan and APxJ/EM equities
![](images/12f779fa7116916260949bcc7bdb5d0be2a28d7044d11bbad24e72f1887b754a.jpg)
Source: EPFR, Morgan Stanley Research

Enhancing return via hedging momentum risk: Our model estimates that Quality style could provide the best diversification against Momentum exposure in both APxJ/EM and

Japan. In addition, if we take the macro backdrop into account, our model suggests overlaying Quality style with "Growth" in APxJ/EM and "Small-Mid Cap Value" in Japan to improve Sharpe ratios. See our Bi-Weekly Perspectives for stock ideas in Exhibit 14 & Exhibit 15.

## Japan Equity Strategy

Daniel Blake, Sho Nakazawa, Makoto Furukawa, Jonathan Garner, Kristal Ji

## Key Views

• We see TOPIX moving further into its secular bull market, with our Dec-24 target at 2,800 (+3% from spot).

• Our bullish stance on Japan is supported by solid earnings, and companies on the TSE Prime Market have reported F3Q24 results with a 20% YoY rise in recurring profits. Total value of buyback programs established in F2024 set a new all-time high amid TSE’s market reforms.

• We expect further inflows from both foreign and retail investors, positioning for stronger nominal growth and corporate governance. Stock purchases through new NISAs marked a new high in January (domestic + overseas stocks) and Sho Nakazawa forecasts NISA inflows to Japan equities to be US\$12bn in 2024.

• We expect an increase in MBO's in Japan and believe the ongoing move toward “going private” is not unrelated to TSE market reforms and the release of METI’s Guidelines for Corporate Takeovers in August last year.

• Our forecast for JPY appreciation to USD/JPY ¥140 by end-2024 underpins our recommendation for unhedged Japan equity exposure and preference for domestics over exporters

## Catalysts and Signposts:

• March 15: First result of Shunto wage negotiations. Our economists forecast 3.7- 4.0% headline and 2.1-2.4% base-up wage increases for 2024.

• March 18-19: BoJ Monetary Policy Meeting – We expect the BoJ to hike rates by 10bp in March, exiting Negative Interest Rate Policy.

## Japan Focus List

• April 1: BoJ Tankan Survey (March).

## China Equity Strategy

Laura Wang, Catherine Chen, Raymond Liu

## Key Views:

• We remain cautious given downward earnings revision pressure as 4Q23 earnings estimates are being revised down notably. We see limited upside toward year-end with our latest price targets for China indices: MSCI China at 53 (-3% vs. market closing prices as of February 29, 2024), Hang Seng at 16,000 (-3%) and CSI 300 at 3,500 (0%). More details here. Fund outflow pressure from local active mutual funds should be largely done, while initial signs of passive inflow turning net positive have also emerged. Patience is still needed before meaningful foreign flows return to China (further details in China Positioning: Active Outflow Cools down; More Patience before Inflow Materializes).

• Continue to prefer A-shares to offshore China, given manufacturing, industrial and clean energy sectors' skew towards the A-share market. We also see bigger flow support for the A-share market, partially due to the potential step-up of government efforts in stabilizing the A-share market in China Positioning: Active Outflow Cools down; More Patience before Inflow Materializes ( Exhibit 49 and Exhibit 50 ). By sector, we continue to prefer select Consumption (with preference for Staples), select Materials and Industrials given earnings certainty), and remain UW Properties and Banks.

• On the SOE theme, we have seen more initiatives from policy makers since beginning of the year. We reiterated that VSCC ("Valuation System with Chinese Characteristics") is a multi-year theme with our newly revisited stock recommendations here ( Exhibit 51 ), while also addressing key questions from investors in China Equity Strategy: FAQs on Our Recent China SOE Reform Report.

• Recommend the following alpha generation investment opportunities: 1) our China/HK Focus List and China A-share Thematic List (see here); 2) SOE reform beneficiaries in our latest SOE insight report; and 3) stock ideas with greater earnings visibility and certainty (see 4Q23 pre-announcements for detailed stock ideas).

## Catalysts and Signposts:

• Policy events: National People's Congress (ongoing), Politburo Meeting (late April), and Third Plenum (date TBC).

• Earnings downward revisions are ongoing and should continue though April/early May. Will likely stay as a negative catalyst given the new record negative net-net pre-announcements we have seen for the 4Q23 cycle.

• Macro data prints: Housing sales, inflation and PMI could help gauge early signs of a macro trough and deflationary pressure clearing up (NBS macro data release schedule).

• Liquidity: US 10-year bond yield trend and USDCNY trend. Domestic liquidity could remain relatively tight due to a lack of credit/monetary multiplier effect (China Equity Strategy: China Free Liquidity vs. MSCI China YoY Change [17 Jan

## Australia Macro+ Strategy

Chris Nicol, Chris Read, Antony Conte, Isabel Black

## Key Views:

• Our end-2024 price target for the S&P/ASX 200 is 7,350, some 3% below current market levels. Valuations have risen over recent months and are at the top end of their historical relationship with bond yields, which we think puts the emphasis on earnings to drive market upside. While our indicators suggest a trough in earnings is forming, a sharper rebound is not evident at this stage.

• We expect economic growth will remain patchy, and continue to see downside risks across both construction and retail sectors. We do not expect the RBA to pivot to easing as fast as the market expects, and have them on hold through 2024 given sticky services inflation, less restrictive policy settings and further fiscal stimulus emerging.

• Our recommended sector positioning remains relatively defensive: overweight energy, healthcare and insurance as well as select quality growth and offshore earners names. UW domestic cycle exposure including banks, consumer and housing-linked.

## Catalysts and Signposts:

• February Reporting Season: We observed a high level of dispersion in sector returns in February as the market continued to set new highs at 7,699pts, +0.9% higher m/m. Technology and Discretionary sectors were strong, Financials added value, whilst Resources were a drag on performance.

• RBA March Meeting: RBA evolution remains key to the domestic cycle, given the sensitivity of the economy to changes in rates. We continue to see a cautious pivot from the RBA.

• Government May Budget: Government policy remains very active, and we see scope for further policy announcements on both the spending and tax sides into the May Budget.

## Corporate Catalysts:

• March 2024, S&P/ASX Quarterly Index Review: On Friday, March 1, 2024, S&P Dow Jones Indices announced changes in the S&P/ASX Indices. This review included the S&P/ASX 20, 50, 100, 200 and 300 Indices along with the All Ordinaries Index, effectively the entire S&P/ASX Index hierarchy was reviewed. Changes are effective close of trading on Friday, March 15, 2024. At this review, we observed a number of changes across the indices including:

QBE replacing NEM within the S&P/ASX 20 Index.

<sub>°</sub> Two changes within the S&P/ASX 100 Index - PME and FLT replacing AWC and RGN.

Within the S&P/ASX 200 Index we observed four changes, with AD8, RED, SDR, SMR replacing CHN, CXO, SYA and WBT.

<sub>°</sub> Across the S&P/ASX 300 Index, there were 14 additions and 10 deletions, to bring the index constituent membership back to 300. Additions include: BMN, DVP, LOT, LRS, MEI, MRM, MVF, NXG, PDI, PFP, RDX, REG, TUA and WC8. Deletions include: AGY, APX, BCB, LKE, LLL, NMT, PBH, SSR, SVR and SWM.

## India Equity Strategy

Ridham Desai, Sheela Rathi, Nayant Parekh

## Key Views:

• India is our key pick across EM markets: Fundamentals are underscored by strong macro stability as a result of improving terms of trade, flexible inflation targeting and stable non-portfolio foreign flows, earnings growth of about 20% annually over the next three to four years led by an emerging private capex cycle, re-leveraging of corporate balance sheets, and unfolding of a structural rise in discretionary consumption and a reliable source of domestic risk capital. These factors have reduced both correlations and volatility of Indian stocks relative to EM.

• Base-case outlook is for an improving absolute return and outperformance to EM: We expect equities to rise into the 2024 general elections, as the market is likely to price in continuity and a majority government. While we are in a macro market (in contrast to a stock pickers' market) the macro trade could peak in 2024.

## Catalysts and Signposts:

• Politics – Actions from the opposition alliance, which create a view that it is becoming a credible contender to the incumbent.

• US stock and bond market cues.

• Oil Prices – While India remains less affected than in the past, a further rise would present headwinds.

• Earnings Strength – Our base case is 7% and 10% ahead of consensus on F2025 and F2026, respectively.

• Monetary Policy – Shallow rate cuts but Fed moves remain uncertain.

• Bond flows further improving the structure of the BoP.

• Rise in net issuances that could moderate the continuing strong domestic bid.

## South Korea Equity Strategy

Joon Seok, Heewon Choi, Michelle Kim

## Key Views:

• On the capital market and corporate reform theme, range-bound trading until elections: From here, we believe policy newsflow will have somewhat less impact on the market until general elections (April 10) – the government expects this to be a more gradual process (Corporate Reform; Work In Process). The focus will be more on external factors such as US macro trends, corporate fundamentals, and geopolitics, in our view.

• On the macro side, our Korea Economist, Kathleen Oh, sees a clear economic rationale for the reform agenda and successful reform is likely to enhance household liquidity and spending power (What The Value-Up Program Means To Households).

## Catalysts and Signposts:

• AGM Season (Mid-to-late March): Focus will be on companies with shareholder proposals from investors.

• General Election – April 10, 2024: Post-election policy direction could be affected by elections results and we are waiting for more clarity on policy pledges from the political parties.

• BoK Outlook: We continue to expect BOK to begin rate cuts in 3Q24 (BoK Review: On the Way to a Dovish Move)

• Other catalysts: WGBI Global Bond Index Inclusion Announcement, MSCI Watchlist announcement, and lifting of short-sell ban.

Corporate Reform; Work In Process (26 February, 2024)

S. Korea Sustainability and Strategy: Disrupting Corporate DNA: Reform Renaissance? (20 Feb 2024)

Upgrade KEPCO to OW: Our materials analyst's new price target of W32,000 implies 36% upside potential. (Korea Electric Power: Into a Sustainable Profit Path; Move to OW)

Upgrade L&F to OW: Our Tech/EV Battery analyst believes that L&F is turning more attractive with 4680 Ni-95% opportunities in 2H24 and\~60% MoM cathode volume increase in January. (Negatives in the Price; OW).

Upgrade GS Retail to OW: GS had strong earnings recovery in 2023, with OP up 44% YoY, but this has barely influenced its share price performance. In 2024, a key contributor for its earnings recovery will be its main business, CVS, which should be better appreciated by the market. (Self-help needed; Upgrading GS Retail to OW)

## Taiwan Equity Strategy

## Terence Cheng, Jenny Ting

## Key Views:

• We continue to see a robust TAIEX performance YTD in 2024, closing at 19,305 as of March 4th, a record high level, given intact fundamentals for exporters with rising visibility after end-market inventory destocking cycles for multiple sectors (both Tech and Non-Tech) concluded with continually increasing trading volume, indicating abundant liquidity. Despite likely near-term volatility into earnings season, a leading cash dividend yield vs. regional peers could still offer some downside support to TAIEX valuation, in our view.

• Our Dec-24 TAIEX target is at 18,500 with a bull case target at 20,000. However, TAIEX performance has been solid post Lunar New Year holidays, up 6.7% over just 12 trading days and surpassing our year-end index target, thanks mainly to positive sentiment on the Tech sector spilling over from the US market during the Lunar New Year period.

• Foreign investors’ participation has also been on the rise with foreign investors holdings of TAIEX up by 2ppt YTD in 2024 (compared with +1.14ppt YoY in 2023, an acceleration of capital inflow to TAIEX).

• Key Risks: We would be mindful about overshooting of the TAIEX in the near-term as the US election becomes more of a focus by investors in 2H24.

## Catalysts and Signposts:

• Foreign investors participate more actively or add net positions on the TAIEX.

• Fed and CBC monetary policy outlook – MS forecasts 100bp Fed cuts in 2024 starting June, with CBC on hold until early 2025.

## Sector Allocation:

• OW: Information Technology, Consumer Discretionary, Industrials

• EW: Financials, Consumer Staples

• UW: Materials and Telecoms

# Singapore and ASEAN Equity Strategy

Wilson Ng, Daniel Blake, Kristal Ji, Aitong Li

## Key Views:

• Singapore: Maintain OW on Singapore given high dividend yields and relatively low risks from global slowdown spillovers, with historical outperformance in EM bear markets. 12mf P/E currently stands at 11.9x, 1.6x lower than its five-year average.

• Indonesia: An unofficial quick count of votes has indicated victory by Prabowo-Gibran (the official results do not have to be released until March 20 at the latest), which is a positive development versus our previous concerns of a drawn-out scenario. We will still need to monitor the cabinet formation process and initial policy priorities, but our sector analysts view the election outcome as encouraging for investment sentiment. See Indonesia: Beyond Elections – How to Position? (14 Feb 2024) for more details. We remain OW Indonesia Financials, while the overall MSCI Indonesia market ranks 7th in our APxJ/EM framework. Indonesia's valuations are trading slightly higher than their usually (modest) premiums to EM, while relative profitability is in line with historical averages (also a slight premium). EPFR data shows investor positioning is OW Indonesia.

• Philippines: Macro risks persist with a high current account deficit, while foreign participation remains subdued. We recommend cherry picking and our ASEAN Real Estate Analyst Wilson Ng prefers Philippines developers, especially Ayala Land (see ASEAN Property: 2024 Outlook: Watch for falling rates (10 Jan 2024) and ASEAN Multi-Industry: Philippine Titans: Ayala and SM (20 Aug 2023)).

## Latin America Equity Strategy

Nikolaj Lippmann, Juan Ayala, Julia Nogueira

## Key Views:

• In Latin America, we lean into four key secular themes that can drive superior EPS growth: 1) digitalization (NU, MELI, GLOB, TOTS); 2) near-shoring (GFNORTEO, VESTA, FIBRAPL); 3) energizing the world (PBR, PRIO, ENELCHILE); and 4) expansion of Brazil's agricultural frontiers (RAIL).

• We are overweight Mexico and Brazil: In Mexico, the near-shoring trend should continue to affect manufacturing growth, creating a demand shock for labor, electricity and manufacturing facilities that in turn will elevate prices and incentivize new investments. In Brazil, we see a very strong case for a longer-term external transformation via the expansion of both its energy and agricultural frontiers, which could add 22-30% to gross exports by the end of the decade, according to our analysts. However, today these are better micro stock-picking themes than macro ones.

• We have a quality bias in our Brazil and Latin America model portfolios: As we worry about the tail risks of higher-for-longer rates in the US and a potential hardlanding scenario in China, we prefer to lean into quality names: our LatAm portfolio has a 22% expected ROE in 2024 vs. 16% for the overall market.

• We expect local and international investors to wait for three factors before turning more bullish Brazilian equities: 1) A clear path toward single-digit rates; 2) signs of economic re-acceleration; and 3) clarity on tail risks both from within (interventionism) and without (China). The latter could potentially be a risk to the commodity outlook and thus to the Brazil base- and bull-case scenarios.

• We worry that a potential delay on the start of an easing cycle in the US could be a headwind for the pace of further interest rate cuts in Brazil and across the region: Our US economist Ellen Zentner believes the Fed should deliver its first rate cut in June, in line with market expectations (for details see Global Economic Briefing: The Global 360)

• Finally, we are underweight Chile (current account imbalances and high China exposure), Peru (lingering political uncertainty coupled with China exposure), and Colombia (heterodox policy mix).

## Recent debates:

In our recent report StratChat: Is Brazil Cheap?, we argue that Brazil is one of the cheapest markets in the world on a forward price to earnings basis standing at just 7.9x for the MSCI Brazil, materially below the 11.8x of EM and 20.7x of the S&P500. However, this is a reflection of just a little more than a handful of names: Petrobras, Vale and the banks. At a sector level, Energy (5 constituents) at 6.3x and Materials (5 constituents) at 6.8x are the only sectors with a PE below that of the aggregate benchmark; however, they account for 40% of the weight of the MSCI Brazil index (48 constituents). We believe Financials, Energy and Utilities appear to be at the valuation sweet spot.

## Catalysts and Signposts:

• Brazil government revenues and spending revisions (March 22 and May 22, 2024): We believe a potential downward revision of the country's fiscal target from a primary deficit of 0.0% of GDP to -0.5% of GDP would signal to markets a loser fiscal policy from the Executive, which could further delay the progression of the current easing cycle.

• Mexico Presidential elections (June 2, 2024): We believe investors will focus on three key factors: 1) energy policy (electricity sector reform and cleanup, and the future vision and priorities around oil company Pemex); 2) fiscal policy (Mexico has been an emerging market standout in recent years, with some late slippage in 2024); and 3) the institutional framework in Mexico that represents.

## STOCK RATINGS

Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy, Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell. Investors should carefully read the definitions of al ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone. In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice. An investor's decision to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

## Analyst Stock Ratings

Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months

Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.

## Analyst Industry Views

Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.
